"""Non-drawing sheet preparation: key, one shared shrink, one shared palette."""
from pathlib import Path
import json
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
im = Image.open(ROOT / 'sheet_raw.png').convert('RGBA')
a = np.array(im)
bg = (a[:,:,0] > 190) & (a[:,:,1] < 85) & (a[:,:,2] > 185)
a[bg] = (0,0,0,0)
heights=[]
extents=[]
for row,col in [(0,0),(0,1),(0,2),(1,0),(1,2),(2,0),(2,1),(2,2)]:
    cell=a[row*im.height//3:(row+1)*im.height//3,col*im.width//3:(col+1)*im.width//3]
    yy,xx=np.where(cell[:,:,3]>0)
    heights.append(int(yy.max()-yy.min()+1))
    extents.append(int(max(yy.max()-yy.min()+1,xx.max()-xx.min()+1)))
east = a[im.height//3:2*im.height//3, :im.width//3]  # East sets the single shared scale.
raw_east_area = int((east[:,:,3] > 0).sum())
sy, sx = np.where(east[:,:,3] > 0)
raw_east_height = int(sy.max()-sy.min()+1)
south = a[2*im.height//3:, im.width//3:2*im.width//3]
sy_s, sx_s = np.where(south[:,:,3] > 0)
raw_south_height = int(sy_s.max()-sy_s.min()+1)
raw_east_width = int(sx.max()-sx.min()+1)
scale = 84/raw_east_width
small=Image.fromarray(a).resize((round(im.width*scale),round(im.height*scale)),Image.Resampling.NEAREST)
b=np.array(small); vis=b[:,:,3]>127
# Approved chameleon palette: distinct material ramps and reserved lens colours.
colours = ['9ff5e6','1fc8b4','0f8a86','0a4a52','fff1a0','ffcc1f','c98a0a','d6d6dc','9d9ba2','58555e','3d3a44','211e27','c4620a','ffd84a','fffbe6','141218']
triples = [tuple(bytes.fromhex(c)) for c in colours]
pal = Image.new('P',(1,1))
pal.putpalette([v for c in triples for v in c] + list(triples[-1]) * (256-len(triples)))
# Match the complete sheet to one palette in Lab; preserve the prompt material ramps.
source_lab = np.array(small.convert('RGB').convert('LAB')).astype(float)
palette_rgb = Image.fromarray(np.array([triples], dtype=np.uint8))
choices_lab = np.array(palette_rgb.convert('LAB')).astype(float)[0]
source_lab[...,1:] = (source_lab[...,1:] + 128) % 256 - 128
choices_lab[...,1:] = (choices_lab[...,1:] + 128) % 256 - 128
distance = (source_lab[:,:,None,:] - choices_lab[None,None,:,:]) ** 2
cost = (distance * [1, 3, 3]).sum(axis=3)
# Lens zones measured on this new raw sheet only. Reserve lens colours so gold
# belly plates cannot acquire eye pixels and lens shades cannot merge into gold.
yy,xx=np.indices(vis.shape)
u=xx/small.width; v=yy/small.height
lens_zone=np.zeros(vis.shape,bool)
for x0,y0,x1,y1 in [(294,548,326,590),(927,548,959,590),(238,974,272,1015),(982,974,1017,1015),(550,977,578,1015),(676,977,705,1015)]:
    lens_zone |= (u>=x0/1254)&(u<=x1/1254)&(v>=y0/1254)&(v<=y1/1254)
rgb_src=np.array(small)[:,:,:3].astype(int)
lens=lens_zone & (rgb_src[:,:,0]>155)&(rgb_src[:,:,1]>80)&(rgb_src[:,:,2]<195)
# Include pale glints in the same bounded lens zones.
lens |= lens_zone & (rgb_src[:,:,0]>220)&(rgb_src[:,:,1]>210)
cost[:,:,12:15]=np.where(lens[:,:,None],cost[:,:,12:15],1e12)
for k in list(range(12))+[15]: cost[:,:,k]=np.where(lens,1e12,cost[:,:,k])
nearest=cost.argmin(axis=2)
rgb = Image.fromarray(np.array(triples, dtype=np.uint8)[nearest])
out=np.array(rgb.convert('RGBA')); out[:,:,3]=np.where(vis,255,0); out[~vis]=0
Image.fromarray(out).save(ROOT/'sheet_finished.png')
(ROOT/'preparation.json').write_text(json.dumps({'raw_heights':heights,'raw_extents':extents,'raw_east_area':raw_east_area,'raw_east_height':raw_east_height,'raw_south_height':raw_south_height,'raw_east_width':raw_east_width,'target_east_width':84,'shared_scale':scale,'finished_sheet_size':small.size,'visible_colours':len(np.unique(out[vis,:3],axis=0))},indent=2))
print((ROOT/'preparation.json').read_text())


"""Squirrel cleanup, adapted from wolf take B: whole-sheet nearest-solid-colour cleanup.
Checks every colour, dark plum and internal leg gaps, not one historical RGB.
Alpha and silhouettes stay fixed.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import json
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from scipy import ndimage
HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent

def clean(px):
    solid = px[..., 3] > 0
    rgb = px[..., :3].astype(float)
    r, g, b = rgb[..., 0], rgb[..., 1], rgb[..., 2]
    bad = solid & (r - g > 25) & (b - g > 25) & (b > r * .65)
    # The mech's near-black is deliberate internal ink, never magenta fringe.
    bad &= ~np.all(px[..., :3] == (20, 18, 24), axis=2)
    good = solid & ~bad
    if bad.any() and not good.any():
        raise ValueError("No clean palette pixels remain")
    out = px.copy()
    if bad.any():
        idx = ndimage.distance_transform_edt(~good, return_distances=False, return_indices=True)
        out[bad] = px[idx[0][bad], idx[1][bad]]
    assert np.array_equal(out[..., 3], px[..., 3])
    # A lone pale rim pixel has no same-colour neighbour in its 8-neighbourhood.
    edge = solid & ~ndimage.binary_erosion(solid)
    # The prompt reserves ink for overlaps inside the body, never the silhouette.
    ink = solid & np.all(out[..., :3] == (20, 18, 24), axis=2)
    rim_ink = edge & ink
    if rim_ink.any():
        donor = solid & ~ink
        idx = ndimage.distance_transform_edt(~donor, return_distances=False, return_indices=True)
        out[rim_ink] = out[idx[0][rim_ink], idx[1][rim_ink]]
    pale = solid & (np.dot(out[..., :3], [0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722]) >= 145)
    lone = np.zeros(solid.shape, bool)
    for colour in np.unique(out[pale, :3], axis=0):
        same = solid & np.all(out[..., :3] == colour, axis=2)
        neighbours = ndimage.convolve(same.astype(int), np.ones((3,3),int), mode='constant') - same
        lone |= same & edge & (neighbours == 0)
    donor = solid & ~pale & ~np.all(out[..., :3] == (20, 18, 24), axis=2)
    if lone.any():
        idx = ndimage.distance_transform_edt(~donor, return_distances=False, return_indices=True)
        out[lone] = out[idx[0][lone], idx[1][lone]]
    return out, int(bad.sum()), int(lone.sum()), int(rim_ink.sum())

if __name__ == "__main__":
    path = HERE / "sheet_finished.png"
    px, count, pale_count, ink_count = clean(np.array(Image.open(path).convert("RGBA")))
    Image.fromarray(px).save(path)
    result = {"plum_or_magenta_pixels_repainted": count,
              "remaining_plum_or_magenta_pixels": clean(px)[1], "lone_pale_edge_pixels_repainted": pale_count,
              "remaining_lone_pale_edge_pixels": clean(px)[2],
              "dark_ink_rim_pixels_repainted": ink_count,
              "remaining_dark_ink_rim_pixels": clean(px)[3], "alpha_unchanged": True}
    (HERE / "defringe.json").write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
    print(json.dumps(result))
"""One simultaneous four-neighbour despeckle pass, from shoebill take C; green and white lens colours kept."""
from pathlib import Path
from collections import Counter
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import json
P=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
LENS={(196,98,10),(255,216,74),(255,251,230)}
def despeckle(a):
    h, w = a.shape[:2]
    out = a.copy()
    fixed = 0
    for y in range(h):
        for x in range(w):
            if a[y, x, 3] == 0 or tuple(a[y, x, :3]) in LENS:
                continue
            nbrs = [tuple(a[yy, xx]) for yy, xx in ((y - 1, x), (y + 1, x), (y, x - 1), (y, x + 1))
                    if 0 <= yy < h and 0 <= xx < w and a[yy, xx, 3] > 0]
            if not nbrs:
                continue
            here = a[y, x, :3].astype(int)
            if any(np.abs(here - np.array(n[:3], int)).sum() < 30 for n in nbrs):
                continue
            out[y, x] = Counter(nbrs).most_common(1)[0][0]
            fixed += 1
    return out, fixed


a,n=despeckle(np.array(Image.open(P/'sheet_finished.png').convert('RGBA')))
Image.fromarray(a).save(P/'sheet_finished.png')
(P/'despeckle.json').write_text(json.dumps({'passes':1,'pixels_changed':n,'lens_colours_preserved':['c4620a','ffd84a','fffbe6']},indent=2))
print('despeckle pixels changed',n)
from pathlib import Path
import json
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from scipy import ndimage
P=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
names=['ne','n','nw','e',None,'w','se','s','sw']
sizes={}; depths={}; edges={}; pixels=[]; border={}
for d in [x for x in names if x]:
 im=Image.open(P/'views'/f'{d}.png').convert('RGBA'); a=np.array(im); m=a[...,3]>0
 yy,xx=np.where(m); sizes[d]=[int(xx.max()-xx.min()+1),int(yy.max()-yy.min()+1),int(m.sum())]
 border[d]=bool(m[0].any() or m[-1].any() or m[:,0].any() or m[:,-1].any())
 pix=a[m,:3]; pixels.append(pix); cs,counts=np.unique(pix,axis=0,return_counts=True)
 lum=pix@[.2126,.7152,.0722]; k=counts.argmax()
 depths[d]={'p05':float(np.percentile(lum,5)),'p95':float(np.percentile(lum,95)),'dominant_colour':'#'+bytes(cs[k]).hex(),'dominant_share':float(counts[k]/len(pix)),'height':sizes[d][1]}
 edge=m & ~ndimage.binary_erosion(m); ec,ct=np.unique(a[edge,:3],axis=0,return_counts=True)
 edges[d]={'edge_pixels':int(edge.sum()),'colours':{'#'+bytes(c).hex():{'count':int(n),'share':float(n/edge.sum()),'share_of_colour_on_edge':float(n/np.all(pix==c,axis=1).sum())} for c,n in zip(ec,ct)}}
# Both per-view and combined audits include every palette colour, including zero edge counts.
allpix=np.concatenate(pixels); cs,counts=np.unique(allpix,axis=0,return_counts=True)
combined={}
for c,n in zip(cs,counts):
 key='#'+bytes(c).hex(); count=sum(v['colours'].get(key,{}).get('count',0) for v in edges.values())
 combined[key]={'count':count,'share':count/sum(v['edge_pixels'] for v in edges.values()),'body_count':int(n),'share_of_colour_on_edge':count/int(n)}
 for v in edges.values(): v['colours'].setdefault(key,{'count':0,'share':0,'share_of_colour_on_edge':0})
(P/'edge-audit.json').write_text(json.dumps({'edge_definition':'opaque pixel adjacent to transparency in four-neighbourhood','combined':combined,'views':edges},indent=2))
(P/'sizes.txt').write_text('\n'.join(f'{d}: {sizes[d][0]}x{sizes[d][1]}, opaque area {sizes[d][2]}' for d in ['s','se','e','ne','n'])+'\nBorder contact: '+str(border))
(P/'depth.txt').write_text('\n'.join(f"{d}: p05 {depths[d]['p05']:.2f}; p95 {depths[d]['p95']:.2f}; range {depths[d]['p95']-depths[d]['p05']:.2f}; most common {depths[d]['dominant_colour']} {depths[d]['dominant_share']:.2%}; height {depths[d]['height']}px" for d in ['e','se','s','n']))
(P/'metrics.json').write_text(json.dumps({'sizes':sizes,'depth':depths,'border_contact':border,'colours':len(cs),'top_five_share':float(np.sort(counts)[-5:].sum()/counts.sum())},indent=2))
for game,filename in [(False,'review.png'),(True,'review-game-size.png')]:
 tile_w, tile_h = (92,72) if not game else (68,48)
 sheet=Image.new('RGB',(tile_w*3,tile_h*3),(128,128,128))

  if game:
   im=im.crop(im.getbbox()); im=im.resize((round(im.width*40/im.height),40),Image.Resampling.NEAREST)

prompt-sheet.txt
"""Slice a generated 3x3 direction sheet into the game's eight views, untouched (Claude, 2026-10-13).

Preston, 2026-10-13: the raw Nano Banana output is better than our finishing, so the pixels stay exactly as
generated; this only cuts and places. Grid sheets come back with each creature facing the centre (the way rotate
sheets do), so the cells map to directions by what they show:

    ne  n  nw
    e   .  w
    se  s  sw

Each creature is found as one connected shape (joined across 2-pixel gaps so a claw stays with its body); a shape
wider than a cell and a half is creatures standing close, cut at the emptiest columns near the cell lines. Each
view goes bottom-centred on the game's canvas.

    python tools/art/slice_grid.py <sheet.png> <out_dir> [--canvas 128] [--feet 104]
"""
import argparse
import os
from collections import deque
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

CELLS = {(0, 0): "ne", (1, 0): "n", (2, 0): "nw", (0, 1): "e", (2, 1): "w", (0, 2): "se", (1, 2): "s", (2, 2): "sw"}


def shapes(m, join=2):
    H, W = m.shape
    grow = m.copy()
    for _ in range(join):
        g = grow.copy()
        g[1:] |= grow[:-1]; g[:-1] |= grow[1:]; g[:, 1:] |= grow[:, :-1]; g[:, :-1] |= grow[:, 1:]
        grow = g
    lab = np.full((H, W), -1)
    n = 0
    for y in range(H):
        for x in range(W):
            if grow[y, x] and lab[y, x] < 0:
                q = deque([(y, x)])
                lab[y, x] = n
                while q:
                    cy, cx = q.popleft()
                    for ny in (cy - 1, cy, cy + 1):
                        for nx in (cx - 1, cx, cx + 1):
                            if 0 <= ny < H and 0 <= nx < W and grow[ny, nx] and lab[ny, nx] < 0:
                                lab[ny, nx] = n
                                q.append((ny, nx))
                n += 1
    out = []
    for k in range(n):
        sel = (lab == k) & m
        if sel.sum() < 300:
            continue
        yy, xx = np.where(sel)
        if xx.max() - xx.min() > 1.5 * W / 3:
            cols = sel.sum(0)
            cuts = [int(c - 12 + np.argmin(cols[c - 12:c + 13])) for c in (W // 3, 2 * W // 3) if xx.min() < c < xx.max()]
            edges = [xx.min()] + cuts + [xx.max() + 1]
            for lo, hi in zip(edges[:-1], edges[1:]):
                part = sel.copy()
                part[:, :lo] = False
                part[:, hi:] = False
                out.append(part)
        else:
            out.append(sel)
    return sorted(out, key=lambda p: -int(p.sum()))[:8]


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("sheet")
    ap.add_argument("out")
    ap.add_argument("--canvas", type=int, default=128)
    ap.add_argument("--feet", type=int, default=104)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    img = np.array(Image.open(a.sheet).convert("RGBA"))
    m = img[..., 3] >= 128
    H, W = m.shape
    os.makedirs(a.out, exist_ok=True)
    got = []
    for sel in shapes(m):
        yy, xx = np.where(sel)
        d = CELLS.get((min(2, int(xx.mean() * 3 // W)), min(2, int(yy.mean() * 3 // H))), "centre")
        spr = np.zeros_like(img)
        spr[sel] = img[sel]
        spr[sel, 3] = 255
        spr = Image.fromarray(spr).crop((xx.min(), yy.min(), xx.max() + 1, yy.max() + 1))
        canvas = Image.new("RGBA", (a.canvas, a.canvas), (0, 0, 0, 0))
        canvas.alpha_composite(spr, (int(round(a.canvas / 2 - spr.width / 2)), a.feet + 1 - spr.height))
        canvas.save(os.path.join(a.out, d + ".png"))
        got.append(d)
    print("sliced %d views: %s" % (len(got), " ".join(sorted(got))))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
"""Run sprite_director's between-view checks on any folder of eight stills, without touching the tool or its worklist."""
import sys
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0, "tools/art")
import numpy as np
import sprite_director as sd

for folder in sys.argv[1:]:
    p = Path(folder)
    views = [sd.load(p / f"{f}.png") for f in sd.FACINGS]
    w = sd.view_jumps(views)
    dips = [sd.FACINGS[i] for i in sd.view_dips(views)]
    lone = max(sd.lone_share(v) for v in views)
    colours = max(len(np.unique(v[sd.mask(v)][:, :3], axis=0)) for v in views)
    pair = lambda k: f"{sd.FACINGS[w[k][1]]}>{sd.FACINGS[(w[k][1] + 1) % 8]}"
    fit = sd.front_back(views)
    print(f"{p.parent.name + '/' + p.name:38s} front/back {fit:.2f} ({'ok' if fit >= sd.FRONT_BACK_NEW else f'under {sd.FRONT_BACK_NEW}'})  "
          f"area {w['area'][0]:.2f} ({pair('area')})  height {w['height'][0]:.2f} ({pair('height')})  "
          f"colour {w['colour'][0]:.2f} ({pair('colour')})  dips {dips or 'none'}  lone {lone:.2f}  colours {colours}")

## 6. Process

- Codex draws, code judges, Aseprite fixes (a layered `.aseprite`, one frame per view), Preston approves. Nothing goes into `assets/` without his yes.
- A fresh `codex.cmd exec` with a written brief costs about 60k to 110k tokens a sheet. Resuming the Claude-jobs chat cost 786,677 tokens for Huskward take F on 2026-11-02, because it carries the chat's whole history: use a fresh exec.
- Image references outweigh words. Take F was given take E as its design picture and came back as take E made squatter, crease lines and oval patches included, against a brief that said otherwise. Give the design in words, or as a picture already at game size. Say the cost first; one image job at a time; two tries at most.
- After the sheet: `slice_grid.py`, `view_check.py`, `sheet_judge.py`, `stage_step.py`, the palette edge audit (fringe), the numbers in section 2, then look at full size and game size.
- Walks stay Retro's. Sky's timing (a 24-frame step, about 3 drawings held 4 to 10 frames, lift set per creature) is a game setting, not sprite art.

"""Sheet judge: pass or fail a creature's eight views and name the views to redo (Claude, 2026-10-28).

Preston, 2026-10-28: make the eight-angle views consistent, with code doing the corrections and Codex's image
generation doing the drawing. The GitHub pipelines that hold eight views together (PerfectPixel Studio, Sprite8,
PixelArt 8-Dir Studio; read for structure only, nothing copied) share one idea this project lacked: an automatic
judge that names the views that drifted, so only those are generated again. This is that judge, built on
measure_views.py. The skill that drives it is .codex/skills/hollowtide-sprites/SKILL.md.

Checks, per view against the set (thresholds in LIMITS):
  files     all eight views, one canvas size
  alpha     no partial alpha (the game's sprites are 0 or 255)
  ground    every view stands on the same foot row
  centre    each view sits on the canvas's middle column
  height    no view much taller or shorter than the others
  palette   the view's colours come from the main view's colours (drift)
  specks    few lone pixels
  mirror    e/w, ne/nw, se/sw are flips of each other (skipped with --asymmetric)
  face      the north view is not the south view again. Whether a back diagonal (ne, nw) shows the face is checked
            by eye: a colour test (face colours = the south view's head minus the north view) was tried on 2026-10-28
            and failed both ways, missing the agile line's Stage III (its eye colours are on its back too) and calling
            Tallowroot's good back views 11 times the face. A wrong check is worse than none.
  turned    the north view is about as even left to right as the south view, and is not a diagonal

The verdict splits in two. FIX: faults code corrects without drawing (alpha, ground, centre, and mirror pairs on a
creature that is the same on both sides). REDO: views only a new generation mends (height, palette, specks, face,
turned, and mirror pairs on a one-sided creature, --asymmetric).

  python tools/art/sheet_judge.py <folder> [--main s] [--asymmetric] [--json out.json]
  python tools/art/sheet_judge.py <folder> --fix <out_dir> [--mirror-from east|west]
  python tools/art/sheet_judge.py --all [--set dungeon]        one line per creature in assets/sprites/arena

--fix never repaints (Preston, 2026-10-13: the raw Nano Banana output beat our finishing). It sets alpha to 0 or
255, moves each view so it stands on the shared foot row and the middle column, and with --mirror-from replaces the
other side's three views with flips of this side's. Exit code 0 when every check passes, 1 otherwise.
"""
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))
from measure_views import DIRS, MIRROR, box, iou  # noqa: E402

ART = Path("assets/sprites/arena")
LIMITS = {
    "partial_alpha": 0.01,   # share of visible pixels with alpha between 0 and 255
    "ground_px": 1,          # foot row spread allowed
    "centre_frac": 0.05,     # of the canvas width
    "height_frac": 0.10,     # of the median height (at least height_min_px)
    "height_min_px": 2,
    "palette_share": 0.85,   # share of a view's pixels near a main-view colour
    "palette_dist": 40.0,    # RGB distance that counts as near
    "specks": 6,             # lone pixels allowed
    "mirror_iou": 0.85,
    "n_same": 0.60,          # share of pixels the north view shares, colour for colour, with the south view
    "north_even": 0.80,      # north's left-right evenness as a share of south's
    "north_diag_iou": 0.93,  # north against a back diagonal, silhouettes aligned
}


def load_views(folder):
    views = {}
    for d in DIRS:
        p = Path(folder) / (d + ".png")
        if p.exists():
            views[d] = np.asarray(Image.open(p).convert("RGBA")).copy()
    return views


def feet_row(mask, need=3):
    """The lowest row with at least `need` pixels, so a stray pixel or a tail tip doesn't set the ground."""
    counts = mask.sum(1)
    rows = np.where(counts >= need)[0]
    return int(rows.max()) if len(rows) else -1


def shift(a, dy, dx):
    out = np.zeros_like(a)
"""Non-drawing sheet preparation: key, one shared shrink, one shared palette."""
from pathlib import Path
import json
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
im = Image.open(ROOT / 'sheet_raw.png').convert('RGBA')
a = np.array(im)
bg = (a[:,:,0] > 190) & (a[:,:,1] < 85) & (a[:,:,2] > 185)
a[bg] = (0,0,0,0)
heights=[]
extents=[]
for row,col in [(0,0),(0,1),(0,2),(1,0),(1,2),(2,0),(2,1),(2,2)]:
    cell=a[row*im.height//3:(row+1)*im.height//3,col*im.width//3:(col+1)*im.width//3]
    yy,xx=np.where(cell[:,:,3]>0)
    heights.append(int(yy.max()-yy.min()+1))
    extents.append(int(max(yy.max()-yy.min()+1,xx.max()-xx.min()+1)))
east = a[im.height//3:2*im.height//3, :im.width//3]  # East sets the single shared scale.
raw_east_area = int((east[:,:,3] > 0).sum())
sy, sx = np.where(east[:,:,3] > 0)
raw_east_height = int(sy.max()-sy.min()+1)
south = a[2*im.height//3:, im.width//3:2*im.width//3]
sy_s, sx_s = np.where(south[:,:,3] > 0)
raw_south_height = int(sy_s.max()-sy_s.min()+1)
raw_east_width = int(sx.max()-sx.min()+1)
scale = 70/raw_south_height
small=Image.fromarray(a).resize((round(im.width*scale),round(im.height*scale)),Image.Resampling.NEAREST)
b=np.array(small); vis=b[:,:,3]>127
# Approved rhino material ramps, with distinct red lens and glint.
colours = ['fff4a8','ffcf1f','d18a0a','7a4a06','d6d6dc','9d9ba2','58555e','3d3a44','211e27','ff3a2a','ffe6d8','141218']
triples = [tuple(bytes.fromhex(c)) for c in colours]
pal = Image.new('P',(1,1))
pal.putpalette([v for c in triples for v in c] + list(triples[-1]) * (256-len(triples)))
# Match the complete sheet to one palette in Lab; preserve the prompt material ramps.
source_lab = np.array(small.convert('RGB').convert('LAB')).astype(float)
palette_rgb = Image.fromarray(np.array([triples], dtype=np.uint8))
choices_lab = np.array(palette_rgb.convert('LAB')).astype(float)[0]
source_lab[...,1:] = (source_lab[...,1:] + 128) % 256 - 128
choices_lab[...,1:] = (choices_lab[...,1:] + 128) % 256 - 128
distance = (source_lab[:,:,None,:] - choices_lab[None,None,:,:]) ** 2
cost = (distance * [1, 3, 3]).sum(axis=3)
# Red is a dedicated lens material. Its pale glint is restricted to red proximity.
from scipy import ndimage
rgb_src=np.array(small)[:,:,:3].astype(int)
red=vis & (rgb_src[:,:,0]>170) & (rgb_src[:,:,1]<125) & (rgb_src[:,:,2]<125)
glint=vis & ndimage.binary_dilation(red,iterations=2) & (rgb_src[:,:,0]>220) & (rgb_src[:,:,1]>170) & (rgb_src[:,:,2]>150)
lens=red|glint
cost[:,:,9:11]=np.where(lens[:,:,None],cost[:,:,9:11],1e12)
for k in list(range(9))+[11]: cost[:,:,k]=np.where(lens,1e12,cost[:,:,k])
nearest=cost.argmin(axis=2)
rgb = Image.fromarray(np.array(triples, dtype=np.uint8)[nearest])
out=np.array(rgb.convert('RGBA')); out[:,:,3]=np.where(vis,255,0); out[~vis]=0
Image.fromarray(out).save(ROOT/'sheet_finished.png')
(ROOT/'preparation.json').write_text(json.dumps({'raw_heights':heights,'raw_extents':extents,'raw_east_area':raw_east_area,'raw_east_height':raw_east_height,'raw_south_height':raw_south_height,'raw_east_width':raw_east_width,'target_south_height':70,'shared_scale':scale,'finished_sheet_size':small.size,'visible_colours':len(np.unique(out[vis,:3],axis=0))},indent=2))
print((ROOT/'preparation.json').read_text())

1. Preston's latest direct instruction.
2. The current project files and actual Godot implementation. Check what is built instead of trusting stale status notes.
3. THE_CONCEPT.md for the current creature, essence, art, and scope rules, unless Preston has changed them.
4. CLAUDE.md for established collaboration preferences. Apply later direct user decisions when they revise those preferences.
5. docs/world/world-bible.md is a living design draft. Names, lore, and creature concepts marked working or proposed are not canon.
6. docs/creatures/reshape-proposal.md, old OneDrive copies, audit reports, chat exports, and research notes are evidence or historical context, not automatic approvals.

If two current sources disagree, state the exact disagreement and preserve both until Preston decides, unless one is clearly an old version.

## Safe collaboration in one working tree

Grok, Claude, and Codex may contribute to the same project and repository. Before editing:

1. Check the current Git branch and uncommitted changes.
2. Read this guide and any relevant project instructions.
3. Name the files you intend to change in your handoff or task plan.
4. Avoid editing the same file at the same time. If work overlaps, sequence it or use separate branches/worktrees.
5. Never discard, reset, or overwrite uncommitted work unless Preston explicitly asks.
6. Keep each task small enough to review. Prefer one finished playable slice over broad scaffolding.
7. Do not commit, publish, merge, install packages, or modify external services unless Preston requested that action.

The assistant making a change owns the final handoff. A second assistant can review the diff and offer specific corrections when asked rather than repeat the entire task.

## Handoff format

Use this short format when one assistant finishes work for the other:

- **Goal:** the requested outcome.

- **Decisions:** what Preston approved, what is still only a proposal.
- **Verification:** what was actually run or visually checked; say plainly when nothing was run.
- **Next safe step:** one concrete action, with any user decision it depends on.
